### F-088 · P2 · UX — Edit sheet keeps an armed 'Delete this expense?' confirmation when the next expense is opened

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/EditExpenseSheet.tsx:24 (confirmDelete never reset), :212-229 (confirm block), :222 (delete toast has no Undo)
- **Impact:** One tap deletes a different expense from the one the user armed, with no way to recover it in the UI on a phone.
- **Fix:** Key the sheet body by expense.id so all local state resets, and add the Undo action to the delete toast via the shared add/undo hook (ARC-17).
- **Evidence:** Chromium :4174 Ledger: open 'Kopi O at kopitiam' -> tap 'Delete expense' -> Close -> open 'Grab to work'. The confirmation 'Delete this expense? This cannot be undone.' is already showing (scratch/out/browser-checks.log editSheetArmedDelete, shots/edit-sheet-armed-delete.png). The store does push an undo entry, but this path's toast has no Undo.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: ARC-16.

### G-002 · P2 · A11y — Keyboard: pressing Enter twice on the invisible swipe 'Delete <item>' deletes two records in the virtualized list (focus stays on the reused button)

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerRow.tsx:63-72 (swipe Delete is first in tab order at opacity 0); src/features/ledger/VirtualLedgerList.tsx:52 (index-keyed slots)
- **Impact:** Laptop keyboard or screen-reader users can chain-delete records. The focused control is invisible and its accessible name changes with no announcement.
- **Fix:** The stable keys from GAP1-01 make the focused button unmount with its row. Then move focus deliberately to the next row's main button, not its Delete. Make the swipe Delete tabIndex=-1 and aria-hidden unless revealed.
- **Evidence:** e_keyboard2.json, desktop 1280x900: focus the third row, then Shift+Tab lands on 'Delete Item 056' (opacity 0). Enter deletes Item 056. Focus stays on the same DOM button, which is now named 'Delete Item 075' and sits beside row Item 075. A second Enter deletes Item 075, and focus moves on to 'Delete Item 094'. Chromium and WebKit behave identically. Control, grouped list (140 rows): focus falls to <body> and only 1 record is deleted.
- **Verification:** code: confirmed (P1); reproduce: confirmed (P2); impact: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP1-02.

### G-003 · P2 · UX — A quick second tap after a swipe-Delete also deletes the next record in the normal (<=150) list: the always-visible trash sits 4-5 px from the swipe-Delete centre

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerRow.tsx:63-72 (swipe Delete w-24 at right-0, centre x=325 at 390 px), :144-154 (always-visible md:hidden trash, 32x32, left edge x=330)
- **Impact:** The owner's Month view (~65 rows, not virtualized) loses the neighbouring expense on a reflex double tap. This is related to the known 'one-tap delete where the thumb rests' issue. It is listed separately because the swipe flow itself parks the thumb on the next row's trash, and the fix (a post-delete tap guard) is not implied by that issue.
- **Fix:** After any delete, ignore taps on the list for about 400 ms. Collapse the removed row with a short height animation so the next row does not land under the finger instantly. On touch devices where swipe is the delete gesture, drop the always-visible trash or move it out of the swipe-Delete footprint.
- **Evidence:** Grouped control in a1 (month, 140 rows): the second tap deleted the next record 5/5 on Chromium and 5/5 on WebKit. Yet elementFromPoint at the tap point returns the next row's Edit body, so this is touch adjustment snapping to the trash. a2_tap_xscan.json, on an un-swiped row: a tap at x=322 (Chromium) or x=326 (WebKit) already deletes, while x<=318 opens Edit. a3_footprint.json: 4/10 positions inside the old Delete footprint delete a second record in grouped mode, against 10/10 in virtual mode.
- **Verification:** code: confirmed (P2); reproduce: confirmed (P1); impact: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP1-03.

### G-009 · P2 · Data — After midnight the Ledger puts the old period's label on the new period's numbers ('October 2026 · $4.00 · 1 entry', 'Jan 1 - Dec 31, 2026 · $4.00')

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:218-250 (dateRangeLabel memo deps [range, specificDate, customStart, customEnd]: no date, no data) vs :105-143 (filtered recomputes on expenses/search)
- **Impact:** These are the wrong numbers attached to a period. The first entry of a new month or year makes the Ledger claim that the owner spent $4 in October, or $4 in all of 2026. The empty state under 'October 31, 2026' suggests the day's entries are gone.
- **Fix:** Derive the label and the filter bounds from one pure helper, `rangeBounds(range, now) -> { start, end, label }`, memoized on [range, specificDate, customStart, customEnd, today] (the `today` from GAP2-01). filtered and dateRangeLabel then cannot disagree. Add a unit test pinning `now` at 31 Oct 23:59 and 1 Nov 00:01.
- **Evidence:** From s2_ledger_rollover.json, on both engines, with the Ledger kept mounted from 23:59. Month range: after typing and clearing a search at 07:30 the list shows '$0.00 · 0 entries · No results for this view' under the label 'October 2026'. After quick-add 'coffee 4' it shows '$4.00 · 1 entry' under 'October 2026' (shots/s2_webkit_oct31_month_after_add.png). Year range on 1 Jan 2027: '$4.00 · 1 entry' labelled 'Jan 1 - Dec 31, 2026'. Today range: '$4.00 · 1 entry' labelled 'October 31, 2026', and before the add, 'No results for this view' under 'October 31, 2026' even though Oct 31 has 2 entries. The label is corrected only by a remount or a range change.
- **Verification:** impact: confirmed (P2); code: confirmed (P1); reproduce: confirmed (P1). Sources: GAP2-03.

### G-010 · P2 · Data — Mixed-clock rendering: labels and highlights read new Date() at render while their data is memoized, giving contradictory screens after midnight and mislabelled past years

- **Where:** src/features/pulse/PulseScreen.tsx:183,191,258; src/features/pulse/MonthlyBars.tsx:9,25-27,82-83; src/features/pulse/Heatmap.tsx:91; src/features/insights/InsightsScreen.tsx:142,237-238,263-266
- **Impact:** Any re-render after midnight (a tap, a toggle) turns a stale but consistent screen into one that disagrees with itself, including a 12x monthly average on 1 January. Past-year bars that look 'future' hide two months of real spending in the comparison.
- **Fix:** Never call new Date() in render. Have the analytics return everything the view draws: monthlyTotals with isCurrent/isFuture computed against the period (none are 'future' in a past year), axis labels taken from data[0], data[14] and data[last], and the forecast start date. Ring the heatmap cell whose date === today (from the clock store). Use the same `now` everywhere through the GAP2-01 parameter.
- **Evidence:** Year view, kept mounted from 31 Dec 23:59, after any re-render on 1 Jan (s1*pulse_rollover.json; shots/s1_webkit_dec31_year_2_morning_rerender.png). The screen shows '2026 · $5,304.00 · Monthly avg $5,304.00 · 1 of 12 months': the average is 12x too high and contradicts 'Avg/mo $442' in the card below. The axis reads 'Jan … Jun … Jan'. The Jan 2026 bar is highlighted as current and Feb–Dec 2026 are dimmed as 'future'. The heatmap today-ring disappears (heatToday [] and the grid still ends at Oct 31 / Dec 31). Insights after tapping a week bar on 1 Nov (s3_insights_rollover.json): the forecast axis shifts to 'Nov 1 – Nov 14' over bins computed from Oct 31, and the trend axis shifts to 'Oct 3 … Today' over Oct 2–Oct 31 data (the offset grows by one day per day). The week chart loses its today highlight. The same code mislabels past years with no rollover at all. At 19 Oct 2026, the 2025 year view highlights Oct 2025 as current, dims Nov 2025 ($435) and Dec 2025 ($448) as future, and ends the axis at 'Oct' (s10_past_year_bars.json, shots/s10*\*\_past_year.png, both engines).
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP2-02.

### G-011 · P2 · Data — A visible Undo silently does nothing after the app has been in the background for a few seconds: the delete or add is not reverted and there is no feedback

- **Where:** src/ui/ToastHost.tsx:19-24 (dismissal by setTimeout, frozen while hidden); src/stores/useExpenseStore.ts:208,237,256 (expiresAt = Date.now()+5000) and :406-417 (consumeUndo drops expired entries silently); src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:161-176; src/features/entry/SmartInput.tsx:62-79; src/app/AppShell.tsx:134-137 (mod+z same path)
- **Impact:** This is the only recovery path for an accidental delete (one tap, no confirm). It fails while appearing to work, so the owner believes the entry is back when it is gone.
- **Fix:** Put the toast and the undo on the same clock. Store createdAt (Date.now) on the toast. On visibilitychange->visible, dismiss any toast whose wall-clock age is >= its duration. Alternatively, let undo validity follow the toast rather than a separate 5 s wall-clock deadline: clearUndo when the toast is dismissed, and keep the entry while the toast is visible. If consumeUndo finds nothing, show an error toast ('Too late to undo') rather than nothing.
- **Evidence:** s5_undo_after_resume.json, Chromium and WebKit. The background is modelled with clock.setSystemTime, which moves the wall clock without firing timers, the same as iOS suspending the PWA. Control with no background: Ledger delete 'Lunch' -> Undo restores it (stored 20 -> 21), and quick-add 'coffee 4' -> Undo removes it (22 -> 21). After 30 s in the background, the toast 'Deleted "Lunch" \| Undo' is still on screen. Tapping Undo leaves stored at 20 (WebKit also dismisses the toast), so 'Lunch' stays deleted. For an add, the toast 'Added "coffee" - $4.00 \| Undo' is still shown, and Undo leaves stored at 22. Nothing tells the user the undo failed.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP2-04.

### G-015 · P2 · Data — Past-period Pulse still shows today's entries and a today-anchored 14-week heatmap

- **Where:** src/features/pulse/PulseScreen.tsx:376-379 (TodayStrip, unguarded in month view), PulseScreen.tsx:327-332 + src/lib/analytics.ts:161-176 (heatmap built from `now`, comment says 'regardless of offset')
- **Impact:** When the owner reviews last month (the main reason to press the back chevron), the last card reads 'Today $27.56' under a DECEMBER header, and the Activity card shows days unrelated to the totals above it, so 'which days did I overspend in December' cannot be answered on Pulse.
- **Fix:** Render TodayStrip only when monthOffset === 0 (or swap it for 'Biggest days in <Month>' on past months). Pass the viewed period into the heatmap: anchor it to monthEnd/yearEnd for offsets > 0 (or show a month calendar grid for the viewed month), and update the '14 weeks' meta to match.
- **Evidence:** Oracle harness, 7 frozen SGT clocks x 13 past months = 91/91 past-month renders show both leaks; 14/14 past-year renders show the today heatmap. Screen 'Pulse month DECEMBER 2026' / clock 1 Jan 2027 00:05 SGT / expected: no Today card, heatmap ending Dec 31, 2026 / shown: 'Today 4 entries $27.56' (the four 1 Jan 2027 rows, incl. the 00:03 kopi) and 'Sep 27 – Jan 1, 2027'. Screen 'Pulse year 2025' / clock 15 Oct 2026 11:55 / expected heatmap within 2025 / shown 'Jul 12 – Oct 15, 2026'. Screenshots: <scratch>/shots/C6-jan1-viewing-dec2026-390.png, <scratch>/shots/year1-hero-and-bars-390.png. Note: the hero 'Today' figure is correctly hidden on past months (PulseScreen.tsx:142, verified 91/91), only the bottom strip leaks. WebKit gives identical results.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP3-01.

### G-016 · P2 · Data — Closed months are judged against today's budgets, with '0d left' countdowns and a red pace ring

- **Where:** src/lib/analytics.ts:122-128,144-158 (current `budgets` map used for any offset; daysLeft forced to 0); src/features/pulse/PulseScreen.tsx:272-275,336-345; src/features/pulse/BudgetActual.tsx:82; budgetUpdatedAt exists (src/stores/useExpenseStore.ts:34, written at :340) but analytics never reads it
- **Impact:** Creating or raising a budget today instantly rewrites history in red, and the 'Nd left' wording implies a live countdown on a closed month. The owner cannot tell whether he actually stayed within the cap he had at the time.
- **Fix:** For offsets > 0 render a 'Final' state: spent vs cap, Over/Under by, no days-left text, no Projected, ring relabelled '% of budget' or hidden. Skip caps whose budgetUpdatedAt is after monthEnd as a best-effort guard. True history needs effective-dated caps (see ideas).
- **Evidence:** Screen 'Pulse month SEPTEMBER 2026' / clock 15 Oct 2026 11:55 / expected: Shopping had no cap in September (cap created 12 Oct; budgetUpdatedAt = now-3d), no 'days left' on a finished month / shown: 'Shopping $478.99 / $200.00 Over by $278.99' in red, meta '0d left in September', '0d left' on every row, pace ring '470%' '0d left'. Clock 1 Jan 2027 viewing DECEMBER 2026: ring '560%', 'Over by $553.60' on the same 3-day-old cap. Counts: 91/91 past-month renders show '0d left in <Month>' and a ring with '0d left'; the 3-day-old Shopping cap alone paints 2-3 of 13 past months red at every clock. Screenshots: <scratch>/shots/C2-15th-1155-pulse-month-1.png, <scratch>/shots/C6-jan1-viewing-dec2026-390.png.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP3-02.

### G-018 · P2 · Data — Past-month hero chart is captioned 'Last 30 days' and leaves out day 1, so its total disagrees with the hero total

- **Where:** src/lib/analytics.ts:178-187 (30 days ending monthEnd); src/features/pulse/PulseScreen.tsx:204 (caption), :233-253 (axis); src/features/pulse/Sparkline.tsx:37-39,112 (sr-only 'Total $X')
- **Impact:** The 1st of the month, a common day for subscriptions and month-start top-ups, never appears in that month's own chart, and February's chart shows January spending. Screen-reader users hear two different totals one line apart.
- **Fix:** For offsets > 0, build `daily` from monthStart to monthEnd (28-31 points). Caption it '<Month> by day' and label the axis with the first, middle and last day of that month.
- **Evidence:** 60/91 past-month renders: chart total differs from the month total (every 31-day month drops day 1; every February pulls in Jan 30-31 or Jan 31). Screen 'Pulse month AUGUST 2026' / clock 15 Oct 2026 11:55 / expected: chart covers Aug 1-31 = $162.55 / shown: 'LAST 30 DAYS', axis 'Aug 2 · Aug 16 · Aug 31', chart total $158.61. 'FEBRUARY 2026' same clock / expected $246.63 / shown $251.06 (window Jan 30-Feb 28). 'DECEMBER 2026' at 1 Jan 2027 / expected $4,651.47 / shown $4,484.62. Screenshot: <scratch>/shots/C2-viewing-aug2026-hero-390.png; full list in <scratch>/checks-sgt.json (field 'spark total = viewed month total').
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP3-04.

### G-019 · P2 · Data — Budget projections extrapolate a single day-1 purchase across the whole month, so ordinary spending turns red at 00:05 on the 1st

- **Where:** src/lib/analytics.ts:123 (daysElapsed = now.getDate(), today counted as a full day), :148 (expected), :155 (projected = total/daysElapsed\*dim); src/features/pulse/BudgetActual.tsx:20-21,76-80 (ratio >= 1.05 means red, AlertTriangle and 'Projected')
- **Impact:** Every month opens with false red alarms on the budget card. Shade logs daily, so the first coffee or Grab of the month trips the alarm, which teaches him to ignore the warnings that matter later in the month.
- **Fix:** Suppress 'Projected' and the red state until a minimum sample exists (for example day >= 5 or a share of the month elapsed), or blend the run-rate with the trailing 30-day or prior-month average. Count today as partly elapsed. Show 'Last day' instead of '0d left'.
- **Evidence:** Screen 'Pulse month OCTOBER 2026' / clock 1 Oct 2026 00:05 / Transportation spent $20.84 (one $18.40 Grab + $2.44 MRT; previous 3 full months $362.10, $3.42, $28.32) / shown: red 'Projected $646.04'; Shopping, one $33.40 order, shows 'Projected $1,035.40'; ring 230%. Clock 1 Jan 2027 00:05: Transportation $20.09 shows 'Projected $622.79'. On day 1 of a 31-day month a row turns red once spend passes 1.05 x cap/31: Transportation $4.06, Shopping $6.77, Food $15.24, Subscriptions $2.03. The same whole-day arithmetic shows '0d left in March' at 09:30 on 31 Mar (clock C4). Screenshot: <scratch>/shots/C1-oct1-0005-budget-projection-390.png. Distinct from F-025, which covers the aggregate ring.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP3-05.

### G-023 · P2 · Code — 'Clear all filters' and the empty-state 'Clear filters' never clear the range, category or day while a search is active: the old filters come back about 0.25 s later

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:100-103 (clearAll), :199-208 (search debounce effect), :195-198 (comment claims the timer reads fresh params, which is false); node_modules/react-router/dist/development/chunk-OB3PAWPO.mjs:6843 (RouterProvider commits navigations inside React.startTransition)
- **Impact:** The only reset control on the Ledger, and the empty-state recovery button, fail in exactly the case where a search is active (the usual reason for 'No results'). The user then sees a total for filters they believe they removed and has to undo each control by hand.
- **Fix:** Move Ledger filter state into one hook (useLedgerFilters). Keep the debounce timer in a ref and clearTimeout it in clearAll. When the timer fires, merge only q into the LIVE URL (new URLSearchParams(window.location.search)), never into a captured params object, and skip the write when live q === draft. Pass { replace: true, flushSync: true } for discrete filter taps. Add a Playwright regression (Year + category + search -> Clear all -> /ledger, Month, All) and a fake-timer unit test for the hook.
- **Evidence:** Deterministic. No tight timing was involved: the search had settled for at least 1 s before the tap. a08*clear_all_and_double_delete*{chromium,webkit}.json shows 0/24 trials fully cleared (3 variants x 4 trials x 2 engines); a03*clear_all_history*\*.json shows 0/4. Event log: replaceState '/ledger' at +1 ms, then '/ledger?range=year&category=Food+%26+Dining' at +226-235 ms on Chromium and +281-328 ms on WebKit. (1) Year + Food & Dining + 'ch', then Clear all: the result is Year \| Food & Dining \| $378.80 / 19 entries, where Month $746.80 / 30 entries was expected. (2) Empty state on Today + 'zzz', then 'Clear filters': it stays on Today, $14.60 / 2 entries. (3) Heatmap day drill (?date=2026-10-19) + 'ch', then Clear all: the day filter returns. Screenshot: shots/a08_year_cat_search_webkit_after_clear.png. Mechanism: setSearch('') is a synchronous update, while setParams' navigation is committed in a transition. The [search] effect is therefore created in a render that still holds the pre-clear params, and 200 ms later it writes them back.
- **Verification:** code: confirmed (P1); reproduce: confirmed (P1); impact: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP4-01.

### G-024 · P2 · Code — A filter tap within ~200 ms of the Ledger appearing (from any entry path) or of the last search keystroke is silently undone: the highlight and total snap back

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:65 (search seeded from q), :199-208 (the effect runs on mount and on every search change, then writes the params captured in that render 200 ms later), setters :69-99
- **Impact:** The first filter choice after a Pulse drill-down, a tab switch or Back reverts about 0.2 s later. The user sees the selection jump back and either taps again or misreads the total.
- **Fix:** Same hook as GAP4-01. Do not write the URL on mount (the initial draft already equals q). The debounce writes only q and merges it into the live URL when it fires. No timer should ever write a whole params object captured earlier.
- **Evidence:** a01 (182 trials, 7 entry paths: TabBar, TodayStrip View, heatmap day, BudgetActual, TopCategories, palette 'Go to Ledger', palette expense; Chromium 390x844 + WebKit iPhone 13). Taps fired by an in-page timer at most 180 ms after the filter bar appeared were reverted in 56/56 trials; taps at 240 ms or later in 0/44. a07 (warm app, both engines): TabBar, TodayStrip and Back-remount reverted 18/18 at 40-180 ms and 0/12 at 240-320 ms. The stale write lands 202-249 ms (Chromium) / 209-283 ms (WebKit) after mount, and ~270-300 ms at 4x CPU with ~640 entries (a tap at +257 ms was still reverted). On a warm app the filter bar appears 38-43 ms after a TabBar tap on Chromium but 198-263 ms after it on WebKit iPhone 13, so on an iPhone the dead zone is ~200-490 ms after the tab tap. A practised 'Ledger, then Today' double tap lands there. What the user sees when a tap is reverted: tab->Week shows Month $746.80; TodayStrip->Week shows Today $14.60; heatmap->Week shows the day $14.60; BudgetActual/TopCategories->Year shows Month $174.50; Back to ?range=year->Week shows Year $2,009.34. Same window after typing. a02: Year/category taps 49-204 ms after the last keystroke were reverted 8/8 (q write at +217-257 ms; +315-357 ms at 4x CPU/~640 entries). a07 R1: tapping the Ledger tab as a reset within 200 ms of typing was undone 6/6 (URL back to ?range=year&q=gr). Playwright-cadence taps on a cold Chromium load: 30/35 reverted.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP4-02.

### G-025 · P2 · UX — The 4 s 'Added ... Undo' toast floats over the Edit sheet. On iPhone, back-dating a just-added expense either drops the date change or hits Undo and deletes the expense, and both report 'Expense updated'

- **Where:** src/ui/ToastHost.tsx:74-77 (fixed, bottom 88px + safe area, z-[100]) vs src/ui/Sheet.tsx:85,101 (z-[96]/z-[97]); src/features/ledger/EditExpenseSheet.tsx:74 (unconditional success toast)
- **Impact:** The most common phone correction (log fast, then fix the day) can silently keep the wrong date or destroy the entry. The app reports success either way.
- **Fix:** While a Sheet is open, move the toast stack above the sheet header (or render it inside the sheet) and keep the toast body pointer-events:none so only its own buttons take taps. Dismiss the 'Added - Undo' toast when the user opens that same expense in the editor, because editing supersedes undo. Pair with GAP4-04 so a vanished record is detected instead of 'saved'.
- **Evidence:** a09_backdate_after_quickadd.json (WebKit iPhone 13 390x664 and Chromium 390x664, real touchscreen taps 1.1-1.6 s after quick-adding 'Kopi 2.5'). All 4 date chips are 100% covered by the toast. Tapping the centre of '3d ago' hits the toast body and the chip is not selected; Save then toasts 'Expense updated' and the entry stays dated today (2026-10-19T04:00Z). Tapping the chip's right edge, which the toast's Undo button overlaps by 213 px2 on WebKit and 109 px2 on Chromium, deletes the expense being edited. The sheet still shows 'Kopi $2.5 Today', Save toasts 'Expense updated', storage holds nothing and the row is gone. Screenshots: shots/a09_webkit_iphone13_undo_overlap_after_chip_tap.png, shots/c_webkit_toast_covers_date_chips.png. a09b sweep (WebKit): the toast covers Edit-sheet controls at 6 of 7 iPhone sizes. These are category chips (375x553), the date chips (375x647, 390x664), Notes (390x797), Pick date (430x739) and Cancel + Save changes (430x932 standalone PWA). Only 390x844 is clear. Desktop/tablet (1280x800, 1440x900, 820x1180): nothing covered (a10).
- **Verification:** code: confirmed (P1); reproduce: confirmed (P1); impact: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP4-03.

### G-026 · P2 · Data — The Edit sheet saves a stale snapshot: a save after the record was removed is a silent no-op toasted as 'Expense updated', and a save after a newer edit from another device overwrites it on every device

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:55, :414, :439, :450 (editing holds the Expense object); src/features/ledger/EditExpenseSheet.tsx:48-58 (form re-seeded only when that object changes), :60-76 (patch writes all 5 fields, success toast unconditional); src/stores/useExpenseStore.ts:225-243 (updateExpense returns silently when the id is gone; stamps updatedAt=now); src/services/syncService.ts:73-75 (last-writer-wins by updatedAt); src/stores/useSyncStore.ts:95-101, 129-139 (realtime pull runs applySync while the UI is open)
- **Impact:** With cross-device sync on, an editor left open silently reverts newer edits on all devices, or confirms an edit that went nowhere.
- **Fix:** Hold editingId instead of a snapshot and read the live record with useExpenseStore(s => s.expenses.find(e => e.id === editingId)). If it disappears while the sheet is open, show 'This expense was deleted' with Restore/Close and disable Save. If its updatedAt changes while the form is dirty, show 'Changed on another device' and re-seed the untouched fields. Patch only dirty fields (diff the form against the values it was seeded from). Make updateExpense return a boolean (or throw) and show an error toast on false. Add store-level unit tests for A/B/C.
- **Evidence:** Browser (a05_edit_stale.json). On desktop Chromium: quick-add Kopi, open its editor, press Ctrl+Z (focus sits on Close, so the global undo runs). The row vanishes behind the sheet and the sheet stays open. Change the amount to 3.2, add a note and Save: the toast says 'Expense updated' and storage holds nothing for Kopi. The same happens on Chromium and WebKit phones via the toast's Undo. Store harness with the real store, mergeBackups and applySync (harness/edit_snapshot.ts -> edit_snapshot.result.json). (A) The phone corrects the amount 4.5 -> 5.4 while the laptop's sheet is open. After the pull the laptop store has 5.4 but the form still shows 4.5. The user only adds a note and saves: the store goes back to 4.5 with updatedAt=now, and the merged payload for every device is amount 4.5. The phone's correction is lost everywhere. (B) The record is deleted on another device while the sheet is open: Save does nothing and toasts 'Expense updated'. (C) In-tab add-Undo under the open sheet: same result.
- **Verification:** code: confirmed (P1); reproduce: confirmed (P1); impact: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP4-04.

### G-027 · P2 · Data — The delete toast's Undo silently does nothing after a sixth quick delete or after the phone was locked, yet it dismisses as if it worked

- **Where:** src/stores/useExpenseStore.ts:245-264 (undo stack .slice(-5); expiresAt = Date.now()+5000), :406-417 (consumeUndo returns silently when the entry is missing or expired); src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:161-177 (no feedback when the entry is not found); src/ui/ToastHost.tsx:19-24, 42-51 (4 s setTimeout lifetime; always dismisses after the action)
- **Impact:** When tidying duplicates in a row, or deleting and then pocketing the phone, the user sees Undo succeed while the item stays deleted.
- **Fix:** Tie each undo entry's lifetime to its toast. Never evict an entry a visible toast references, base expiry on toast dismissal rather than Date.now, and on visibilitychange->visible dismiss toasts whose entries have expired. When consumeUndo finds nothing, show 'Too late to undo'. Collapse consecutive deletes into one 'Deleted 6 - Undo all' toast so the stack never covers half the list.
- **Evidence:** a06b_undo_eviction.json (both engines): 6 row deletes 200 ms apart stack 6 toasts 316 px tall (top at 260 of 664 px on WebKit iPhone 13, 440 of 844 on Chromium; screenshot shots/d_chromium_six_delete_toasts.png). The oldest toast's Undo gives restoredCount 0 and the toast disappears. The next toast's Undo gives restoredCount 1 (control). a06_delete_undo.json U2 (both engines): delete, then move the wall clock +6 s while timers are paused (phone locked or backgrounded). The toast still offers Undo, and tapping it restores nothing. src has no visibilitychange handler (grep).
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP4-05.

### G-030 · P2 · UX — Ledger search shows 'No results' / $0.00 after a predictive-bar pick (trailing space) or an iPhone curly apostrophe

- **Where:** src/features/ledger/LedgerScreen.tsx:132-139 (filter), 342-359 (input)
- **Impact:** On a phone, the normal way to search is to type 3-4 letters and tap the suggestion. iOS QuickType, iOS 17+ inline prediction and Gboard all append a space after the accepted word, so whole-word searches for names that end with that word (Spotify, Netflix, Grab rides…) fail. The user sees a false 'No results' and a $0.00 total and has no visible cause to fix.
- **Fix:** Normalise both sides before matching: const q = normalizeText(search).trim().toLowerCase(), where normalizeText applies NFKC, folds ‘’‛′ to ' and “”″ to ", and collapses whitespace. Apply the same normalizeText to itemName, category and notes. Consider matching whitespace-split tokens with AND so 'grab camp' finds 'Grab to camp'. Add enterKeyHint='search' and type='search' while doing this (see GAP5-06).
- **Evidence:** The filter is q = search.toLowerCase() followed by itemName.toLowerCase().includes(q), with no trim and no punctuation folding. Measured on the demo build at 390x844 with range=all (<scratch>/search-log.txt): 'spotify' gives 3 rows, 'spotify ' and 'Spotify ' give 0 rows plus the empty state ('No results for this view', screenshot search-trailing-space-spotify.png, where the space is invisible). 'netflix' gives 3, 'netflix ' gives 0. "mcdonald's" gives 1, while 'McDonald’s' (U+2019, what iOS Smart Punctuation types) gives 0 (search-smart-apostrophe.png). The command palette's Fuse search returns the right hits for the same three queries, so the two search surfaces disagree.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP5-02.

### G-031 · P2 · Data — OS autocorrect is left on for item names: Singlish words are rewritten (teh→the, kopi→copy, mee→me, prata→Prada) and each rewrite becomes the stored name and a new learned mapping key

- **Where:** src/features/entry/SmartInput.tsx:95-114; src/features/entry/CommandPalette.tsx:218-227; src/features/entry/AddExpenseSheet.tsx:156-164; src/features/ledger/EditExpenseSheet.tsx:87-93; mapping learn in src/stores/useExpenseStore.ts:200-204
- **Impact:** The Ledger, palette and recurring detection show the wrong item names. Categories are wrong whenever the rewrite misses the fuzzy threshold. Learned mappings accumulate junk keys, which widen mis-filing over time. The rate is highest on a new install or a new phone, before the keyboard learns kopi/teh; it is lower but non-zero for a long-time user, since iOS still corrects 'teh' and 'mee'.
- **Fix:** On the quick-add input, the palette input, Add 'What' and Edit 'Item', set autoCorrect='off' and spellCheck={false}. Keep autoCapitalize='sentences' or set 'none'; capitalisation is harmless, so choose whichever display style you prefer. Leave Notes as they are, since prose benefits from autocorrect. Replace what the predictive bar offered with an app-owned suggestion row built from the user's own history (see ideas). Only learn a mapping key when the category came from an explicit user choice or an exact match, not from a fuzzy hit below about 0.9 confidence.
- **Evidence:** A DOM scan (attrs.cjs, <scratch>/attrs-log.txt) found no autocorrect, autocapitalize, spellcheck, autocomplete or enterkeyhint attribute on any of 13 text-entry control types (44 rendered instances on the demo phone run), and none inside a <form>. So the iOS Safari/PWA defaults apply (autocorrect on, sentence auto-capitalisation, QuickType bar, Smart Punctuation, '.' shortcut), and so do Gboard's on Android (autocorrect, auto-cap, auto-space after a suggestion, double-space period). The real parser and store were bundled with esbuild and fed plausible autocorrect output (build/corpus.ts, corpus-output.json), with these results. 14 of 15 autocorrect variants were mangled. 'copy 1.80' was filed as Other even though 'kopi' is mapped to Food & Dining, and 'The'/'The peng'/'Me pok'/'Prada'/'Pirate'/'Mile'/'Car fan' were saved under those names. Autocorrect alone taught 10 distinct junk mapping keys (the, the peng, the c, copy peng, me pok, me poke, prada, pirate, mile, car fan). Those keys then mis-file unrelated entries: 'car wash 15' goes to Food & Dining (0.50), 'fan 25' to Food & Dining (0.90) and 'parade 5' to Food & Dining (pollute-output.txt). On the Add-sheet path the second matcher sends 'The' to Other while quick-add sends it to Food & Dining (sheetcat-output.txt). 'Mart 2.10' (mrt autocorrected) gets parsed as 2 March with no amount, a case of the already-reported month bug. Auto-capitalisation alone is harmless: 12 of 12 capitalised variants parsed correctly (keys are lower-cased).
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP5-03.

### G-032 · P2 · Code — Quick-add rejects the sentence punctuation that phone keyboards and dictation insert: 'Grab 12.' disables Add while still showing 12; 'Grab. 12' / 'Grab, 12' save 'Grab.' / 'Grab,'

- **Where:** src/utils/parseExpense.ts:22 (AMOUNT_RE lookahead (?=\s\|$)), 90-94 (item name used verbatim)
- **Impact:** Triggers are iOS's default '.' shortcut (double-tap space), Gboard's double-space period, and iOS 16+ dictation auto-punctuation, which is the natural hands-free way to log while carrying something. All of them end the text with '.', so the entry silently fails to add, or saves a polluted name and mapping key. The UI contradicts itself: the number is visible, yet it asks for an amount.
- **Fix:** Before extracting tokens, strip trailing [.,;:!?]+ from the whole input and from the item name, and drop a trailing comma after the item. Relax the amount lookahead to (?=[\s.,;:!?]\|$). Treat 'dollars' / 'bucks' / 'sgd' after a number, and a leading 'for' before an amount, as currency or filler words. Add the corpus rows from this audit as a table-driven test in src/utils/parseExpense.test.ts, which has only 3 cases today.
- **Evidence:** AMOUNT_RE requires whitespace or end-of-string straight after the number, so any trailing '.', '!', '?' or ',' kills the amount. Corpus (corpus-output.json): all 4 trailing-period variants ('Kopi 1.80. ', 'Grab 12. ', 'Chicken rice 4.5. ', 'Yesterday grab 12. ') were not committable. Both mid-period variants saved 'Kopi.' / 'Grab.' and learned the keys 'kopi.' / 'grab.'. Dictation-shaped input (dictation-output.txt): 'Kopi $1.80.', 'Kopi, $1.80.', 'Grab $12.', 'Chicken rice for $4.50.', 'Grab to camp $14.', 'Kopi 1.80!' and 'Kopi 1.80?' were all not committable (7 of 10). 'Grab 12 dollars.' gave item 'Grab dollars.', and 'Grab, 12' gave item 'Grab,'. In the browser (returnkey-log.txt, return-quickadd-period.png), 'Grab 12. ' plus Return stored nothing and gave no feedback, while the preview shows the pill 'Grab 12.' next to an orange 'add amount'.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP5-04.

### G-035 · P2 · Data — Connect treats an unknown sync code as success: a one-character typo (or a pruned row) silently starts a new, empty relay row and shows 'Connected — data synced'

- **Where:** src/features/settings/SyncPanel.tsx:111-128; src/stores/useSyncStore.ts:130-132; src/services/syncService.ts:201-211; supabase/migrations/002_sync_indexes.sql:28
- **Impact:** Re-linking is the owner's main recovery path. One mistyped character turns it into a silent split: the phone and the laptop both show Synced while logging into different rows. Because nothing in SyncPanel shows what the row contains or when another device last wrote to it, the owner cannot tell. A wiped phone at least looks empty. A phone that already had local entries looks normal, and the split can go unnoticed for days.
- **Fix:** Separate joining a group from creating one. setupSync(code, { mode: 'join' \| 'create' }): 'join' (the Connect button) must find an existing row. If pullSync returns null, show an error ('No synced data found for this code. Check it or generate a new one') and do not save the code. Before committing a join, show a preview from the pulled payload: '~640 entries, last change 2 min ago. Join?'. Only Generate may create a row. To cut typos, add a QR code or check characters. In the linked state, show 'Last change from another device: <time>' so being alone becomes visible.
- **Evidence:** handleConnect only checks the UUID regex (SyncPanel.tsx:114) and then always shows toast.success('Connected — data synced') (:122). triggerSync does `remote ? mergeBackups(local, remote) : local` (useSyncStore.ts:132), so when pullSync returns null (maybeSingle, syncService.ts:204-210) it pushes the local payload and creates a new row. Harness (real stores, fake relay) r4-typo.log: wiped phone C enters a code one hex digit off the laptop's. Result: 'toast.success Connected — data synced', C {expenses: 0, cloudStatus: 'synced', lastSyncAt set}. The relay now holds two rows (89ab: ~640 entries, 89ad: 0 entries). After 3 rounds of logging on both devices: laptop 644, phone 3, both show cloudStatus 'synced', and neither device ever sees the other's entries. The same path runs for a correct code whose row was removed by prune_stale_sync_buckets (90 days untouched).
- **Verification:** code: confirmed (P1); impact: confirmed (P2); reproduce: confirmed (P1). Sources: GAP6-01.

### G-037 · P2 · UX — Re-linking a wiped (or any new) device hides the owner's custom categories behind 'More…' for good, and makes a dead renamed default the first chip

- **Where:** src/services/syncService.ts:81-83 (category union keeps LOCAL order first); src/features/entry/AddExpenseSheet.tsx:184-207 (only categories.slice(0, 8) become chips); no reorder UI anywhere in src
- **Impact:** After a phone wipe, reinstall or Safari storage eviction, the owner's most-used categories need the small 'More…' select on every one-handed entry, and the first chip files entries into a category he deleted. (That 'Food & Dining' comes back at all is the known category-sync issue. This finding is about the chip order on the re-linked device.)
- **Fix:** In mergeBackups, when the local list is still the untouched DEFAULT_CATEGORIES (or the local device has 0 expenses and has never synced), adopt the remote order. Otherwise keep remote order for shared names and append local-only names. Better for one-hand use and immune to merge order: build the 8 chips from usage (most-used in the last 60 days, then the list order), and add a simple 'pin to chips' / reorder control in Settings → Categories.
- **Evidence:** A fresh device's local list is the 11 DEFAULT_CATEGORIES. mergeBackups keeps the local order first, so the phone's merged list is defaults first, custom categories last. Harness r4-wiped-relink.log: laptop order [Food, Transportation, Army, 家庭 Family, Gaming, ...]. Wiped phone after Connect: [Food & Dining, Gaming, Social & Leisure, Transportation, Shopping, Healthcare, Grooming, Subscriptions, Investment, Travel, Other, Food, Army, 家庭 Family, ☕ Coffee]. Browser render (WebKit, iPhone 13, shots/r7-add-sheet-relinked-phone-state.png, r7.json): the phone's 8 Add-sheet chips include none of the owner's 4 custom categories, and 'Food & Dining' (renamed away on the laptop) is the first chip. Every later merge on that phone keeps its local order, and the app has no way to reorder categories. JSON import keeps the order (r2: categories identical), so only the sync re-link path is affected.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP6-03.

### G-038 · P2 · Data — Clear all and Import have no safety net: no automatic snapshot, no undo, no 'what you will lose' count, and import is replace-only even though the app recommends export→import between devices

- **Where:** src/features/settings/SettingsDrawer.tsx:420-425 (confirmImport), :452-458 ('Multiple devices: Export a JSON backup here, then import it on the other device'), :462-481, :521-526; src/stores/useExpenseStore.ts:267-274 (clearAllExpenses sets undoStack: []), :357-371 (importBackup replaces every slice)
- **Impact:** A mistaken import of an old backup, or a mis-tap on Clear all (most painful with sync off or before linking), destroys data with no way back. Following the app's own multi-device instructions overwrites any entries made on the receiving device since its last import.
- **Fix:** Before importBackup or clearAllExpenses, store exportBackup() as a 'pre-action snapshot' (IndexedDB preferred; about 115-165 KB at ~640 entries). Show a toast with 'Undo' for 10 s and a 'Restore data from before <action>' row in Settings → Backup for 7 days. In the import confirm, show a diff from mergeBackups-style id comparison: 'This backup: ~640 entries up to 19 Oct. Your current data: 642 entries, 1 newer than the backup will be lost.' Offer 'Merge into current data' (reuse mergeBackups) next to 'Replace'. Rewrite the 'Multiple devices' copy to point at Merge or Sync.
- **Evidence:** Real UI run (Chromium, 390x844; r5.json, shots/r5-\*.png): seed ~640 entries, quick-add 'kopi peng 2.4' (642), Import the ~640-entry backup. Count goes to ~640, quickAddEntrySurvivedImport=false. The import toast is a plain toast.success('Imported N expenses') with no action (SettingsDrawer.tsx:423); the only Undo button on screen after import (r5.json undoButtonAfterImport=1) belongs to the earlier quick-add toast. Then 'Clear all expenses' → 'Yes, delete all': count 0, 0 Undo buttons (toast.info('All expenses cleared') with no action, SettingsDrawer.tsx:525); deletedIds grows to 761. localStorage keys are identical before and after both actions (no snapshot key), sessionStorage is empty, there are no IndexedDB databases, and no download is triggered. The confirm copy says only 'This will replace all current data. This cannot be undone.' It does not say how many current entries exist or how many are newer than the backup.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP6-04.

### G-039 · P2 · Security — No way to rotate a sync code or erase the cloud copy: Disconnect→Generate leaves other devices on the old code and the old row (full history) on the relay indefinitely

- **Where:** src/features/settings/SyncPanel.tsx:98-109, :151-231 (Generate only offered when unlinked), :349-374 (Disconnect only clears the local code); src/stores/useSyncStore.ts:104-117; supabase/migrations/001_sync.sql:35-36 (anon has no DELETE); 002_sync_indexes.sql:21-28 (prune is manual and only for rows untouched 90 days)
- **Impact:** If the owner believes his code leaked (screenshot, chat, lost phone), the only lever the app offers just forks his own devices: both still show Synced, and the old code keeps working with his full spending history on it. There is also no 'forget my data in the cloud' when he stops using sync. (That the relay is readable by anyone holding the anon key is a separate known finding.)
- **Fix:** Add 'Change sync code' while linked: generate NEW, push the merged payload to NEW, then write a small `{ movedTo: NEW, movedAt }` tombstone payload to OLD. Other devices that pull OLD see movedTo and offer to move (or move automatically) after a confirm. Add 'Remove cloud copy' (a payload-blanking tombstone now; a SECURITY DEFINER delete RPC once the relay hardening lands). Show the 'other devices stay on the old code' consequence in the Disconnect and Generate copy.
- **Evidence:** Harness r4-generate-while-linked.log: laptop A and phone B linked; A taps Disconnect then Generate. A {syncId: NEW, cloudStatus: 'synced'}, B {syncId: OLD, cloudStatus: 'synced'}. 'A sees B-after: false \| B sees A-after: false'. Final relay: OLD row 642 entries, NEW row 642 entries. The client cannot delete the old row (no DELETE grant), and while B stays linked it keeps touching the row, so the 90-day prune never applies.
- **Verification:** combined: confirmed (P2). Sources: GAP6-05.

## Improvement ideas

Ideas from all auditors, sorted by recommendation and then user value. Near-duplicates from different auditors are kept so each source shows.

| Rec   | Value | Effort | Data model | Idea                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Source                       |
| ----- | ----- | ------ | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------- |
| BUILD | 5     | S      | additive   | "Left to spend" and a safe daily allowance on the Pulse hero (optional overall monthly budget)                                                                                                             | The question a daily logger actually asks is "can I afford this today?". Pulse shows only what has been spent; the remaining amount and per-day allowance are never shown. This also gives the pace ring meaning (PRD-03).                       | product                      |
| BUILD | 5     | S      | none       | Quick picks: one-tap "repeat" chips for frequent or recent entries (item + amount + category)                                                                                                              | Most daily spend repeats (kopi, Grab, MRT top-up, Netflix). Logging one should take a single tap, done with the thumb from the home screen.                                                                                                      | product                      |
| BUILD | 5     | M      | none       | Amount-first entry sheet: decimal keypad focused first, sticky Add, chips sorted by usage, live category pre-select, and a "÷ split" helper that logs only your share                                      | This removes 2 or more taps and two reaches per log and makes the phone flow work one-handed. The split helper covers the common case of paying for a group and getting PayNow paybacks, without building full split accounting.                 | product                      |
| BUILD | 5     | M      | additive   | End-to-end encrypted, gzip-compressed sync payloads (WebCrypto AES-GCM + CompressionStream)                                                                                                                | This makes the relay zero-knowledge. Even if rows are read again, they are ciphertext, and a forged payload fails AES-GCM authentication, so tombstone injection is impossible. Gzip also cuts the payload about 5x, so the 1 MB row limit stops | security                     |
